# Rules → formal rules

Every ownership rule of [`docs/MEMORY.md`](../docs/MEMORY.md) and the
phase that formalizes it. A rule is **done** when its typing rule is
stated, used in the soundness proof, and every hole probe it is meant to
reject is mapped to it by its `// map:` line (the table `corpus/map.tsv`,
written from those lines by [`tools/fuzz/holes/map.sh`](../tools/fuzz/holes/map.sh)).

Formal rules are Lean names in [`A1/`](A1/) and [`A2/`](A2/) (the same
names, extended): typing rules are constructors of `StmtT`, `RhsT`,
`PushArg` and the call check `CallT`; `kill`, `exitScope` and `derive`
are the context operations they use. A1's calculus has owned `Vec` trees
(a `String` is a `Vec` of bytes); A2 adds structs, enums, closures and
trait objects, panics and the drop journal; threads are A3.

| MEMORY.md | Rule | Phase | Formal rule | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| §1, §7.6 | Single owner, drop flags, auto-drop | A1 | an owner's slot is `⟨own, some tree⟩`, a released or moved one `⟨own, none⟩` (the drop flag); `HeapOK` (one owner per allocation); `Step.scopeSkip` + `DropSlot`; `exitScope` | done |
| §2.1 | Moves — use after move | A1 / A2 | `RhsT.moveOwn`, `PushArg.move`, `CallT` (`sink`): the moved binding is dead (`live := false`) and every read needs a live entry; a moved argument is passed nowhere else in the call and reached from no other argument. A take out of a place that may be replaced (`RhsT.take`, `EvalR.take`, Agora #826: an owner or an `inout .replace` parameter, of a `Vec` whose elements hold no views): the `Vec` moves out, the place is killed as a replace kills it and keeps a fresh empty `Vec`, live and holding nothing — nurlc's hand-over of an `inout` handle parameter (Agora #776) | done |
| §2.1b | Releasing what the compiler releases | A1 / B | `StmtT.free` releases only a live owner and kills it; inside `unsafe` is B | done for safe code |
| §2.2 | Second names | A1 | `RhsT.moveOwn` (an owner moves), `RhsT.copyBor`, `RhsT.copyRoot`, `RhsT.borrow` (a borrow is copied and depends on the root, `derive`) | done |
| §2.3 | Stack references must not escape | A1 / A2 | for borrows and views: `exitScope` ends them with their root, `FnOK` lets a result depend only on parameters; a closure's captures by handle are what it depends on (`RhsT.pack`: `D`), by name what it holds (`capDeps`), ended with their binding (`exitScope`, `kill`) | done |
| §2.4 | Exclusive access for `inout` arguments | A1 / A2 | `CallT`: an argument that moves or may change is not lent out (`F`), is passed once, and is reached from no other argument; `paramOK`, `canDo` (the `inout` bound); an `inout` argument of any owning type is its owner, never a borrow or a copy (`paramOK_inout`, h295), and a summary pair on one names a kind inside it (`kindFits`) | done |
| §2.5 | Iterator invalidation | A1 | a borrow of `xs` depends on `xs`: `StmtT.push` (`kill .realloc`), `RhsT.pop` (`kill .drop`), `StmtT.assign` and `StmtT.free` (`kill .replace`) end it | done |
| §2.6 | Loop-carried moves | A1 | `StmtT.while`: the body ends in the context it starts in | done |
| §2.7 | Escape through a callee | A1 / A2 / A3 | `CallT` against the callee's summary: what the result borrows (`lends`) and holds (`rholds`), what the callee stores into `inout` arguments (`outHolds`), its effect bound on each; `FnOK` checks the body against it; a closure handed to a callee is held by its result (`heldOfArgs`) and run as the callee may (`NamesFree`, `mutNames`); threads are A3 | done |
| §2.8 | Escape through a return | A1 / A2 | `FnOK` (the result depends only on what `lends` / `rholds` name), `callRes` at the call; a call through a package lends from the receiver too (`recvRes`) | done |
| §2.10 | Views end when their source may reallocate | A1 / A2 | `RhsT.view`, `RhsT.sat`; `StmtT.push` (`Eff.realloc` ends views, and element borrows when elements hold views); `StmtT.editB` (a push, a truncation or an element set through a borrow kills every memory root of its path: `.drop`, or `.realloc` for a push through a same-typed `.self` copy); `PushArg` / `held` (a view in a container is still a view); `outHolds` (a callee storing a view into an argument) | done through an owner, an `inout` binding, or any borrow of elements that hold no views (`StmtT.editB`, agora #295); what stays outside is in `CLAIM.md` |
| §2.12 | A stored value belongs to its owner | A1 / A2 | `PushArg.move`, `CallT` (`sink`); aggregate literals (`RhsT.pair`, `RhsT.inl`, `RhsT.inr`) move or copy what they store, a field taken out (`takel`, `taker`, `unpair`) moves; a field replaced in place is the struct taken apart (`StmtT.unpair`, an owner or an `inout` struct it may replace) and assigned again (`StmtT.assign`), ending every borrow of the struct | done |
| §2.13 | A raw string in a value is a view | A1 / A2 | a string view is a `slice` entry (`RhsT.view`) depending on its owner; a view stored in an aggregate or a package makes that value depend on what it views (`ViewsOK`, `held`) | done |
| §2.11, §7.5 | Closures hold their captures | A2 | `RhsT.pack` (`CapsOK`: captures by handle are depended on, by name held, each read or changed within its bound); `StmtT.callc` (the call through a package: `CallT` on the code with the captures as `inout` arguments of their own, `NamesFree` — a binding a closure may change is changed by the call and reached from no argument but by a closure that changes it the same way — and no argument is such a binding); `sigCtx` / `symNames` (a closure a callee is handed may change the callee's `inout` parameters); `ValOK.pkg` / `envDeps` (the environment); `A2/CAPTURES_DESIGN.md` | done |
| §7.6 | A destructor (`% Drop`) runs at its binding's scope end, normal or panic path | A2 | `StmtT.letd` / `StmtT.scopeD` (a binding with a destructor, `DtorOK`: one `in` parameter, an integer result, lends and holds nothing); `Step.scopeDEnd` (the destructor's call, then `scope x` frees the tree), `Step.scopeDMoved` / `scopeDPMoved` (a moved binding runs none), `Step.scopeDP` (the unwind stops at the frame and leaves it at its end, `seq (scopeD x f skip) panic`: its end, `scopeDEnd`, runs the destructor, then the panic is raised again; Agora #790: what the unwinding orphaned is reaped inside the frame first); `unwindCtx`, `Inv.pinOut` / `PinOK`; a type that holds views: `letd` arms the binding (`Entry.arm`), `DtorSafe` / `DbindSafe` / `EndOK` keep its views alive, `MInv.armed` keeps them valid while its slot owns a value, `Entry.revive` hands them to the destructor | done, views included (A2-drop.1, A2-drop.2: agora #221, #241); on the type, not the binding: A2-drop.3 (agora #253) |
| §7.2, §7.3 | Panics and the drop journal | A2 | the panic steps unwind to `StmtT.catchR` (`recover`: `StmtT.letrec`, `recStmt`) through the journal, dropping what each scope owns (`A2/Panic.lean`); `no_leak_end` (`A2/Leak.lean`) | done |
| §7.7 | Reference-count cycles | B | `Rc` and its collector are the standard library's `unsafe` base | not started |
| §6.5 | Threads: `Send`, `Sync`, `Arc`, `Mutex`, `Locked`, `RwLocked` and `% Frozen`, `Atomic`, `PFile`, channels | A3 | A3.0, threads that only move: `SpawnOK` (a spawned function takes only `sink` parameters of types that hold no views), `TStep.spawn` (the arguments move to the new thread, the spawner's slots are left empty, through A2's `CallT`), `Touch` / `Race`, and the configuration invariant `CInv` (each thread A2's `ThreadOK` on its own part of the address space) — `A3.claim`. Then each shared kind as a block: `Arc` (A3.1: `RhsT.arcNew` / `arcGet`'s frozen lend, `ThruOK.noShare`, the sweep), `Locked` and `Mutex` (A3.2: `withLock` / `scopeL`, `Blocked`, `Rhs.lockTake` / `lockRestore`, `StmtT.lockedGet` / `lockedSet` / `lockedApply` / `lockedAwait` / `mutexLock`), `RwLocked` and `% Frozen` / `% NotSend` (A3.3: a read is a view block, `Stmt.withRead`, `Rhs.rwTryRead` / `rwTryWrite`, `Stmt.rwGet`, `NoGuard`, `Step.scopeLP`, `Step.waitPanic`), `Atomic` and channels (A3.4: `Rhs.atomic`, `Rhs.chanSend` / `chanRecv` / `chanClose`), library handles (A3.5: `Val.cell`, `SendCheck`, `TStep.spawnFail`, `CellInv`, `StmtT.withCellIn`, `Race := AddrRace ∨ CellRace`), closures that cross (A3.5b: `Ty.fn ms q`, `Cross`, `StmtT.callcE`), trait objects (A3.5c, by A3.5b's rules: a trait's bound is its objects' class, `TraitBound`, `TraitAt`, `StmtT.callc_bound`, `ImplOK`, and `SendCheck` walks the box), module globals (A3.6: `Cfg.gl`, `Rhs.glob`, `Ty.sync`, `GlobsOK`, `Made`, `A3.claim_made`) | done (`A3.claim`, `A3.claim_made`; `A3/DESIGN.md` and `CLAIM.md` list what stays outside). Probes: `race_shared_vec`, `thread_reads_freed`, `chan_send_then_use`, `slice_crosses_thread`, `thread_captures_owned_string`, `thread_closure_capture_used_after_spawn`, `closure_run_after_its_thread_copy_took_the_capture`, `spawned_closure_arm_payload` (A3.0); the `Arc`, lock, library-handle, closure, trait-object and global probes name their rules in `A3/DESIGN.md` |
| §3, §6.4 | `unsafe` boundary and the trusted base | B | — | not started |

## nurlc forms the calculus reads as forms it has (phase C)

Phase C proves that every program nurlc accepts is well-typed in the
formal rules. For a few of nurlc's forms that proof goes through a
reading of the form as one the calculus already has, not through a rule
of its own. These are the readings for a change to a Vec's or a String's
elements made through something other than its owner (Agora #295, step
0):

| nurlc form | Read as | Formal rule |
|---|---|---|
| A function that changes the elements of a Vec or String it takes as a plain parameter, as the standard library's mutators (`vec_push`, `vec_set`, `vec_clear`, `string_push_*`) take theirs | an `inout b` parameter, `b` the most it does: `realloc` if it only grows it, `drop` if it may drop elements, never `replace` (a plain parameter is not rebound) | `CallT`, `paramOK_inout`, `callPostAux` (the kill by the bound) |
| A change through a copy of one owner's whole handle (`: x ( id vs )`, `: ~ c vs`, a cursor), to its elements only | the same change made on that owner | `StmtT.push`, `RhsT.pop` |
| A change through a field path of an owner (`( vec_push [u] . o kids x )`) | the struct taken apart, its field changed, the struct put back (§2.12) | `StmtT.unpair`, `StmtT.push`, `StmtT.assign` |
| `vec_clear`, `vec_truncate`, `vec_insert`, `vec_remove` | loops of pops and pushes; `vec_remove v i` directly (`RhsT.remove`, A2-drop.3b; any index since Agora #607) | `RhsT.pop`, `RhsT.remove`, `StmtT.push` |

A reading holds only where nurlc's summary of the callee is at least what
the callee does: Agora #315 was a hole in that summary. A replacing
callee (`inout replace`) given a copy is no reading: the replacement
lands in the copy's own slot, so nurlc refuses it (h295, h367–h370). A
change through any other borrow (an element of a Vec of Vecs, a field
viewer, a join of borrows, a call's lent result) needs no reading:
`StmtT.editB` types it (Agora #295, step 5). What stays outside is in
`CLAIM.md`.

## Changes to MEMORY.md and what they do to the proof

Every change to the rules of `docs/MEMORY.md` is recorded here with its
effect on the formal rules: whether it touches a rule that is proven
(**done** above), and whether it **extends** the calculus (a new rule, or
a rule made to reject more: every existing proof still holds) or
**breaks** a proof (a rule made to accept more, which reopens its lemmas).

Each row lives in a file of its own, `formal/rules/t<N>.md` after its
change's Agora task (`t0.md`: the changes that had none), and the table
is written from those files in task order by `tools/formal_rules.py`
(Agora #299). A change adds or edits its own file and never the table,
so two changes never edit the same lines. The release regenerates the
table: until then, a change made since the last release is in its file
only.

<!-- BEGIN change rows: generated by tools/formal_rules.py from formal/rules/t*.md -->
| Change | MEMORY.md | Touches a proven rule? | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| A closure over a stack binding by pointer handed to any call that detaches it — `thread_spawn_owned`, `spawn`, `spawn_owned`, `spawn_joinable`, `signal_install`, as `thread_spawn` already was — is rejected: what runs it may outlive the frame (h503–h505) | §2.11 | no: a closure kept past its frame is the escape `CallT` already refuses for a keeping callee | extends: rejects more |
| A field of a call's result handed to a callee that takes it is a copy, unless the field left with the read (the call handed its result over, h266); one that cannot be copied is rejected (h424, h425) | §1, §7.6 | no: a lent value is a borrow, which is copied, not moved (`RhsT.copyBor`) | none to the proof: the compiler brought into line with A1 |
| A standard-library struct with its own `% Drop` (the state behind a handle) is sealed: a literal of it or a write to its fields is raw memory, as for a struct with a raw-pointer field (h444, h445); a library helper that needs its caller to size its operands or check its offsets is `raw` (h436–h438, h447–h470) | §3, §6.4 | no: the trusted base is B, not started | extension: safe code is rejected more; B takes the library's sealed structs and `raw` helpers as part of the base |
| A value that needs a drop — an option or a result of a String as well as a handle — kept by a callee is stored in the container it is handed with, so reading it after the container is released is a use after move (h421–h423) | §2.12 | no: a stored value belongs to its owner (`PushArg.move`, `held`) | extends: rejects more |
| A `~` loop's container (a binding, a field of one, or the struct a field it iterates belongs to) given a new value in the body, handed to a callee that takes it, or reached by a closure the body runs that assigns it or hands it to a changing or taking callee is rejected (h416, h418–h420) | §2.5 | no: a borrow of `xs` depends on `xs`, and an assignment or a release ends it (`StmtT.assign`, `StmtT.free`: `kill .replace` / `.drop`) | extends: rejects more |
| A field of an `inout` parameter handed to a callee that takes it is a replacement of that field in the function's summary (an `s` edge of `resolve_replaces`), so the caller's borrows of it end at the call (h417) | §2.4 | no: an `inout` argument that may change is not lent out (`CallT`, `F`) | extends: rejects more |
| A parameter whose value the function moves on through a cursor over it — an option parameter's payload handed back by a `??`, through a local, in a generic — is an inferred `sink` too, not only kept (h408–h415) | §1 | no: a payload taken out (`takel`, `taker`) moves the option, and a `sink` argument is dead after the call (`CallT` (`sink`)) | none to the proof: the compiler's summary brought into line with A1's `CallT` |
| A parameter whose field the function hands to a callee that takes it is an inferred `sink`: the function already owned it (kept) and dropped the rest, and now its caller's binding is moved by the call (h389–h393, h396, h397) | §1 | no: in the calculus a field taken out (`takel`, `taker`, `unpair`) moves its struct, and a `sink` argument is dead after the call (`CallT` (`sink`)) | none to the proof: the compiler's summary brought into line with A1's `CallT` |
| A field handed to a callee that takes it, from a binding whose copy alone the compiler would empty — a `vec_at` copy, a field viewer, a closure's capture — is rejected (`pendfx` / `pendfc` rows); one of a `vec_get` element, an option payload, a cursor or an `inout` parameter is emptied where it lives (h394, h395, h398–h400) | §7.6 | no: in the calculus only a live owner moves (`RhsT.moveOwn`); a borrow is copied (`RhsT.copyBor`) | extends: rejects more |
| A field handed to a parameter that takes it ends the views of its binding as a field replaced does, not as elements changed (h401–h404) | §2.11, §2.12 | no: a borrow or a capture by handle depends on its root (`derive`, `RhsT.pack`), and a field taken out moves the struct | extends: rejects more |
| A field a closure gives a new value through its by-reference capture releases the old value under the binding's drop flag (carried by the env); a binding that only borrows its value cannot be given one there (h405–h407) | §2.11 | no: a capture by name is held by the closure (`RhsT.pack`, `CapsOK`), and a field replaced in place is the struct taken apart and rebuilt (§2.12) | extends: rejects more |
| What a returned value that may hold a closure lends (`retclo`) is read off its source expression at the `^`, through the frame's closures, views and borrows (a bound closure, a join, a reassigned binding, a captured view), not only a literal capturing the parameter by name (h384–h388) | §2.11 | no: in the calculus a returned package's dependencies are its captures' (`RhsT.pack`, `FnOK`), whatever names them | none to the proof: the compiler's summary brought into line with A2's `FnOK` |
| A view of a call's temporary argument is used inside a call that consumes it; kept past it (bound, returned, assigned, stored, taken by a join, handed to a callee that keeps it) it is rejected once the caller owned the temporary — it was left owned by nothing and leaked (h378–h381) | §1 | no: the calculus names every argument (a temporary is a binding its statement makes, and its view borrows it) | extension: safe code is rejected more; the compiler brought into line with A1's named arguments |
| A closure's env is in the panic journal: each function's closure bindings as one frame-table entry (value and owner slot), an argument's closure temporary and a struct literal argument's owned fields while the call runs (h375–h377) | §7.2 | no: A2's panic step drops what each slot owns, a package's environment with it | none to the proof: the compiler brought into line with A2's panic step |
| An assignment's value is lent to whatever reads it (the arm of a `?` / `??` statement it ends drops nothing); where a join that may select the target lends, the value is copied in (h371–h374) | §7.6 | no: in the calculus a conditional is a statement (`ite`) whose arms are assignments, and `= x x` moves the value over itself | none to the proof: the compiler brought into line with the calculus's reading of a join assigned to a binding |
| A binding in a borrowed state is refused as an `inout` argument where the callee may replace that parameter (it used to be released under the binding's owner); a `: ~` copy of a `% Drop` struct from a local owner takes the owner's drop flag, so a replacing call makes the copy the owner and the source moved; a `^` checks its expression's calls first (h367–h370, h295 folded in) | §2.4, §7.6 | no: the calculus has no borrowed binding as an `inout` argument (`paramOK_inout`) | none to the proof: the compiler brought into line with `paramOK_inout`, which never admitted the shape |
| `arc_get` lends every `Arc`'s payload, a trait object's and a cycle-capable one's too (it used to copy those), and the frozen view it lends is tracked for every `Arc`; a method called through a trait object is summarised as the union of its impls (what it changes and what it replaces, an `inout` receiver changing the box) before the summaries resolve (h364–h366) | §6.5, §7.7 | no: a call through an interface (`callc`) takes its parameter modes from the interface, which `Implements` holds every impl to; `Arc` is A3 | none to the proof for the `dyn` call: the compiler's summary had taken less than the impls do, and now takes their union, as `Implements` does. An `( Arc %Trait )` read is accepted where it was a compile error: the same frozen lend A3 must cover for every `arc_get` |
| An owned binding with a drop flag is journaled with the flag, which the drain reads (it was read when the binding was recorded): a value moved into a literal, a `sink` parameter, a closure's capture or a `dyn` box is not released again by a later panic (h361–h363) | §7.2 | no: A2's panic step drops what each slot owns when the panic runs, and a moved owner's slot (`⟨own, none⟩`) owns nothing | none to the proof: the compiler brought into line with A2's panic step |
| A crossing to another thread (`thread_spawn`, `spawn*`, `chan_send`, a pool's return, a `Locked`'s value going in and out) checks at run time that it leaves no owner of a handle whose copies are one state behind, and panics otherwise; a trait object's box is checked through its vtable (h349–h355, h360) | §6.2, §6.5 | no: threads are A3 | closes the open hole recorded below (a library handle's copies on two threads) with a run-time check rather than a rule: A3 must take the crossing's precondition, every owner of such a block goes along, from the check, as a panic where it fails |
| A `% Sync` marker on a `Locked`, a `Channel` or a `Pool` no longer vouches for its value, which faces Send where the container is reached from several threads; an `RwLocked`'s value faces Sync (h357, h358) | §6.5 | no: threads are A3 | extension: safe code is rejected more |
| A binding read out of a library handle field, consumed or taken by a thread, takes its struct along: the struct is dead until given a fresh value (h348, h356) | §7.6 | no: library handles are the trusted base (B) | extension: safe code is rejected more |
| An `inout` parameter given a binding whose value another owner keeps (a field read, a closure's capture) is given a copy; a closure's `inout` parameter is never an owner of its own (h345–h347) | §7.6 | no: the calculus has no borrowed binding as an `inout` argument (`paramOK_inout`) | none to the proof: the compiler gives such a parameter an owned copy, which the calculus types as a fresh owner passed `inout` |
| The journal's elision knows the runtime's panicking entries (`nurl_panic_index`, a CPU tier's `_missing` gate, the crossing check), so a function whose only panic is one of them keeps its registrations (h359) | §7.2 | no | none to the proof: which calls may panic is the implementation's to know |
| A trait object `%Trait` is Send only when its trait declares `: Send`, Sync only with `: Send Sync`; a `: Send` / `: Sync` supertrait is answered by the derivation at each impl (it used to demand an impl block of the marker, which never exists) (h344) | §6.5 | no: threads are A3 | extension for trait objects: safe code that moved or shared one is rejected more; a `: Send` / `: Sync` supertrait is accepted where the derivation holds, which only lets the object cross. A1 and A2 have no threads, so no lemma moves; A3 must take a trait object's thread facts from its trait's promise, since the object's type names no field |
| A positive `% Send` / `% Sync` outside the standard library is `unsafe % Send T { }`; `% NotSend`, `% NotSync`, `% Frozen` and `% Resource` stay safe (they only add checks); the `in` / `sync` / `nocapture` claims and the standard library's own markers are in the trusted base, and `--unsafe-report` lists the program's | §3, §6.4, §6.5 | no: threads are A3 and the trusted base is B, neither started | extension: safe code is rejected more. A1 and A2 have no markers and no threads, so no lemma moves; A3 takes an `unsafe % Send` / `unsafe % Sync` as a hypothesis of the base, as B takes an `unsafe` body |
| `dist/heartbeat.nu`'s thread holds the node's liveness (an `Atomic` and a copy), not the table; `PkMemberTable` is `% NotSend`; `mqtt_listen` takes its client (`sink`); one open hole listed: a library handle's copies on two threads | §6.2, §6.5 | no: threads are A3 | standard library only (no rule changes) and a stated exception to the guarantee; A3 must model library handles' sharing copies (a copy is a second owner of one state), where the open hole sits |
| A destructor that panics while a panic's journal drains is caught by the same `recover`; the first message is kept; the interrupted drain is ended by the frame that catches it | §7.2 | no: A2's drops run no program code (`DropSlot`) | none to the proof: runtime behaviour outside the calculus, stated in `CLAIM.md` (destructors as code are a planned refinement) |
| Formal model: `inout` of every owning type (a struct, an enum, a closure, not only a `Vec`), a field replaced in place (an `inout` struct taken apart and assigned again), summary kinds on such a parameter inside it | §2.4, §2.12 | yes: `paramOK`, `FnOK`, `StmtT.unpair` (rules of A2, done) | extension of the calculus to programs it did not express before: `paramOK` and `FnOK` accept more parameter types, `unpair` more modes (`canDo .replace` for `own`), `FnOK` asks `kindFits` of summaries. Proof-breaking for the lemmas that took an `inout` parameter to be a `Vec` (`AbsOK`, `allowed_canDo`, `callPostAux_lift`, `rr_sub`, `rhs_move`, `CtxWF.unpair`, the `unpair` cases of preservation, progress and renaming): each re-proven, `check.sh` passes |
| `Fiber` is a sealed (`raw`), move-only handle with its own drop: `fiber_join` takes it (`sink`), a joinable handle dropped unjoined detaches its fiber (`nurl_fiber_detach`), and a handle from `spawn` / `fiber_current` is not joinable (h610–h615) | §2.1, §3.3d | no: threads are A3 and the trusted base is B | standard library only (no rule changes): the existing move and sealing rules now apply to the fiber handle; A3 must model a joinable fiber's block as owned by its one handle |
| A select waiter is a counted (`Shared`) block: a channel it is armed on holds a share until the disarm, and the `?? { }` lowering polls once before it makes a waiter (h616) | §3.3d | no: threads are A3 and the trusted base is B | standard library and lowering only (no rule changes): the select machinery called from safe code no longer leaves a raw address behind |
| A handle field of a sealed representation (`. key d`, at any depth of a field chain, or a binding read out of one, or a copy of that binding) handed to a callee that may change it in place — a container mutator's first argument, a summary that changes, reallocates or takes the argument, or a function of the program compiled later, decided by its final summary at module end — is a write to that field, refused outside `unsafe` (h459, h467, h617–h620, h638) | §3.3d | no: the trusted base is B, not started | extension: safe code is rejected more. A1 and A2 have no sealed representations, so no lemma moves; B must state a sealed struct's invariant as kept by every call its fields reach |
| A closure's copy of the handle of a value with no Clone (a Vec of `% Drop` values, an option or result of one, a trait object, what a captured closure holds — what adopting cannot copy in, `\~!(…)` / `\~*(…)`) stays a copy of the frame's handle when the closure is stored into an owner: returning that owner, or storing it into storage the caller holds, is the escape of a view of a released binding (h680–h688) | §2.11 | no: in the calculus a package's dependencies are its captures' (`RhsT.pack`, `FnOK`) and nothing copies them away | none to the proof: the checker's model of an adopted closure narrowed to what adopting really copies, as A2 has it |

A view a closure gives one of its captures, which #259 made a dependency where the closure ran (the closure effect `@vp=`), is refused since agora #312 | §2.10, §2.11 | `inout`: no, A2 already makes it a dependency (`CallT`'s `outHeld`); closures: refused, as A2 refuses it: a closure may not give a capture or its own `inout` parameter a view (`CapsOK`: a changed capture holds no views; `Implements`: no code outHolds on a capture) (agora #272, #312) | an extension for `inout`; for closures, rejects more: the gap is closed by refusal |
| A store that replaces a field a local reader still views hands the reader the old value instead of dropping it. Identity is decided at run time: the struct's first field that has one, else a library handle's control block. The reader then owns the value, and its consumers (literal, push, `sink`, return) move it. They zero the field only while it still holds that value (h196, agora #265) | §2.12 | no: the replace is §2.12's take-apart-and-rebuild, with the old field moved into the reader's owner cell (DynCell, #185): own on the hand-over path, a borrow of the field elsewhere | none to the proof: per-path ownership, as DynCell encodes it |

| A binding read out of a field — a field viewer — that takes the field over (consumed, or handed to a parameter that takes it) ends every borrow of a path overlapping that field: another viewer of it, a viewer of a field of it or of one holding it (agora #271). A binding bound from a field viewer by name (`: R b a`) views the same field, with that field's path, so it may be handed over as the viewer itself may and takes the field (and, for a library handle field, the struct, h348) with it | §7.6 | yes for the first part: two viewers of one field were each a borrow of it, and consuming one left the other a copy of a freed handle (a heap-use-after-free); the second part only moves a binding from `borrow` to `fborrow`, which the first part covers | the first part extends (rejects more); the second relaxes (accepts handing the re-bound viewer to a parameter that takes it) with the same take-over codegen already emits for the viewer it was bound from (#268) |

| A field a `sink` takes (`( take . h buf )`, a field path of a binding) is moved until it is given a value again. A store to the field, a new value for the binding, or a new declaration of its name makes it usable again. A take in a loop's body stays at the head of the next round and after the loop when the binding was bound before the loop (__bck_fmv_carry, agora #351) (agora #280; probes vec_field_taken_by_a_sink_then_pushed, string_field_taken_by_a_sink_then_pushed, struct_with_a_taken_field_handed_on_whole, struct_with_a_taken_field_returned_whole, inout_field_taken_by_a_sink_then_used_by_the_caller; compiler/tests/diag_moved_field, diag_moved_field_inout; agora #351: field_taken_by_a_sink_in_a_loop_then_taken_again, field_taken_by_a_sink_in_a_loop_left_by_break_then_pushed, taken_field_returned_itself, taken_field_returned_in_a_literal, struct_given_a_literal_of_its_taken_field). A store to the field is the struct assigned again (`StmtT.assign`), and an `inout` parameter is live at the body's end (`FnOK`) | none to the proof: an extension (rejects more). The shapes still accepted are outside the calculus and stay open in open/t280.md. A `% Drop` struct's destructor runs on the empty field, where A2's `unpair` runs none |

The walk evaluates that expression with the final summaries (`bck_sx_mut_roots`: what a call lends of its arguments, a part of one marked `^`, a join's arms) and sweeps once for each storage it reaches, as the row naming that binding would; an element root is swept as a `pendlend` row. Inside a closure body the effect is marked lent on the captures the expression names, and replays as `pendlend` where the closure runs or is handed on; an exclusive call reads such an argument as the element it is (`bck_sx_mut_storage`). | A copy of a value that holds views holds the same views (agora #362, #368, #377, #347). What a function makes of what its argument holds is derived from its walk, as #422 derives what it makes of the argument's storage: a parameter that holds views (an owner of them, a Slice, a struct of views) starts its walk holding a pseudo source of its own, `%p`, standing for the caller's views. A value handed back holding them answers `%k` (g_fn_rviews), and another parameter left holding them answers `h>%k` (g_fn_vkeeps; `%K.H` rows at the call). The caller asks what a copy of argument k holds, its views and not its storage (`g_sxe_held`): an owner's dependencies, or what a borrow's sources' elements view. `mem_dup` answers `:%0`, and publishes its operand. A callee with no walk answer (trusted or library code, compiled) whose result can hold views holds what its non-closure arguments hold, and so does a match payload of such a call. A copy taken by the call it is an argument of still holds what it held. A copy of a part of an argument (an element or a field handle, a slice of it; `retpart`) holds what the part holds, not the storage the part is in, and a holder that borrows its value depends on what that value holds, its borrow tying it to the rest. A value that views its own storage — an element viewing a sibling's String, a field viewing another field, a struct of that kind moved in whole — holds views of itself (`vsv_`, recorded where its dependency on itself is dropped): what a copy of a part of it holds includes it. A view field of an owner copied whole (`mem_dup`'s operand, an owner parameter's `%k`, a `%K.H` row: `g_sxe_hctn`) is copied itself, so the copy points where the field does; a view argument itself holds what the elements of its storage view (where it points is its lends'), and a callee that copies the elements of a view it is given — `g_fn_vcopies`, a parameter that is a view of a container (`g_fn_eparams`) — holds those. What a parameter holds given to a string global is refused in the function, as a view parameter's own storage is, and so is a view of it handed to another thread; when the parameter's own value crosses too (it escapes there whole, or a closure that captured it does), its views go as an owner's do, which agora #469 leaves open. A bare view handed back holding it answers `%k`. A parameter the body may give a new value (`inout`, owned) holds `%p` only on the paths that do not: `%p` is a dependency the joins and a loop's dependency snapshot follow. A callee with no walk answer of views (trusted, library or `unsafe` code, compiled) leaves each `inout` argument whose type holds views holding what its non-closure arguments hold (`h>%k`). Recursions, cycles and `dyn` impls settle these answers as they do #422's (`__bck_walk_scc`, the virtual callee's union; a `dyn` decorator's other impls answer `h>%k` too) | §2.13 | yes: made to reject more (a copy, a popped element, a clone or a wrapper's copy of views carried none of them); `vec_map` with a closure that hands back fresh values is now refused where the result outlives the views of the Vec it maps, and so is the element a replace-style library callee (`vec_replace`) hands back where it outlives the views of the value that replaced it, as A2 refuses both (`heldOfArgs` takes every argument) | none to the proof: A2's `callRes` gives a callee's result `heldOfArgs` (what any argument holds), and `PushArg` / `outHolds` give a holder what the value stored holds; `callPostAux` gives each `inout` argument whose type holds views `heldOfArgs` too. nurlc derives those per argument from the callee's walk (`%k`, `h>%k`), a refinement of A2's union, and falls back to the union itself where there is no walk to read, for a result and for an `inout` holder alike. A view of an argument's own storage that a trusted callee keeps is its summary's `outHolds`: its author's word (MEMORY.md §3) |

Before this, the head's dependencies — the ones the loop was entered with — were joined into the exit of every loop (#413), so a holder repointed before every `break` of a `~ T` loop was refused as still viewing a local that had ended (agora #453; probes holder_repointed_*_while_true_* / *_while_one_loop, guards holder_kept_on_* / holder_viewing_again_*) | §7.6 | no: a loop with a `break` is outside A2 (it has no `break`); a precision change: it accepts more | A2 has no `break`. Its `while` leaves at its invariant (`StmtT.while`: the body takes Γ to Γ, and the loop is typed Γ → Γ), and `while (.lit n) s` with `n ≠ 0` is never left from its head (`Step.iteF` needs `Eval m (.lit n) 0`). nurlc's `break` is outside A2's syntax, so no proven lemma reopens. The change rests on the fact that a loop whose condition cannot be false is left only by its `break`s (its exit block is `unreachable` otherwise), which is part of what A2 does not model yet (`break` / `continue`; phase C, `README.md`) |

| A function's result is journaled wherever code of the program's own can run between its evaluation and the `ret`. On a return through a `;` defer chain it is stored into the function's result slot first and that slot is journaled (`mem_ret_guard_into`; a fresh raw string by its address) until the chain's end has read it back (fn_cleanup); a fresh raw string handed back is journaled by its address, and a closure handed back — a literal or a call's, whose environment is fresh (docs/MEMORY.md §7.4) — by a slot holding its environment, while the locals are dropped (`mem_ret_guard_raw`). A panic in a defer body or a local's destructor then releases the result once. No checker rule changes (agora #620) | §7.2 | no: codegen only. The call's result frame (`resFrame`, Agora #611) for the result kinds and the exit path #612 left out | none to the proof |
| A value given a new one leaves its slot before its destructor runs. Where the old value's drop runs code of the program's own (`__drop_runs_code`), `= b ( new )` reads the old value's drop condition, copies the value out of the slot (`mem_udrop_take_old`), stores the new value and sets the binding's flag for it, and only then drops the copy; a field (`= . h f ( new )`, a nested field, a `Vec`, option or closure field) and an `inout` parameter the same, their drops held until the store (`__fs_late_emit`). A panic in the old value's destructor then finds the binding holding the new value, which the unwind drops once, and the destructor's receiver glue releases what the old value still holds. Before, the old value was dropped in place: the unwind dropped it a second time (heap-use-after-free) and lost the new one. A drop that runs no such code goes in place, as before. No checker rule changes: what is accepted and refused is the same (agora #621) | §7.2 | no: codegen only. The calculus refuses `= x v` over a binding that may own a destructor value (`Stmt.assigns`: its `assign` drops the old value without running code) and takes a program that moves the old value to a `letd` and binds the new one; that is the order nurlc now keeps where the destructor runs code — the old value out of its owner first, then its destructor | none to the proof |
| `vec_retain` judges every element before it drops one. The kept elements are swapped to the front, slot for slot, so the `Vec` holds each of its elements exactly once whenever the predicate runs; then the length goes down to the kept ones and the others are dropped through `mem_drop_range` (`nurl_vec_drop_range_dtor`, the record `vec_truncate` and `vec_clear` use). A predicate that panics leaves a `Vec` the drain drops once per element; a destructor that panics leaves a `Vec` of the kept elements, and the others still to go are dropped all the same. Before, each element that went was dropped inside the loop with the `Vec` at its old length, and a panic's drain dropped the `Vec` over slots already given up. No checker rule changes: what is accepted and refused is the same (agora #624) | §7.2 | no: a library change (stdlib/core/vec.nu). `vec_retain` is outside the calculus, as `vec_truncate` / `vec_clear` over destructor elements are (`A2.Dtor.edit_drops_dtor_free`, Agora #607); its drops now go the way theirs do (Agora #608) | none to the proof |
| A function whose returns hand back a lending call's result on one path (a via return whose callee lends, or a lend of a parameter's part or field) and a value of its own on another (a literal or a copy, a call that lends nothing, a binding of one) answers per call (`__hown_mixed`, at module end): every path stores its answer, none a literal `false`, so it was a static lender and its fresh results were never released (agora #625, #739). A temporary of a non-copyable `% Drop` type the callee may hand back whole is dropped on the runs the call's answer says its result is its own (agora #457's rule, extended from handles and options) | §7.6 | no: releases what leaked; the borrow walk's answers are unchanged (a per-call result may still be a lend) | none |

| A value block passed as an argument, ending in a call, is that call's result for what the argument leaves the caller to release after the call (`mem_arg_temps` is asked with the block's effective token). Asked as a block, a result the tail call made was released by nobody. What the block bound lives to the end of the enclosing scope, so a lend out of it is good for the call. The argument is then a temporary of the statement where the tail call may make its result, so a view of it kept past the call is refused as for the unblocked call (`: s d ( string_data { ( pick x y ) } )`, as `( string_data ( pick x y ) )` is) (agora #628 item 1) | §7.1 | no: releases, and rejects more only. A kept view of a block argument whose tail call may hand back a value of its own was accepted; where it did, the value leaked so that the view stayed good | A2 has no value block or arm value as a form of its own: a block is its statements with the tail as the value. Kept: a call's temporary argument is a `letv` around the call, dropped after it, and a view of it does not outlive it — the block argument's tail call result is that temporary, as for the unblocked call |
| `= p { … ( g … ) }`, p an `inout` parameter: the old value is dropped and the result kept by the block's tail call's own answer (rhs_eff_tt), as for `= p ( g … )` — the block was taken for no call, the old value and the result leaked (agora #628). And a binding of a join of bindings (`: String e { … ? c s t }`) handed to a `sink` leaves only the source it holds, by the value's identity (mem_alias_src_leaves), as a store or a return moving it on does (agora #628 item 5) | §7.6 | no: §7.6's assignment rule, applied through a value block | none |

| fn_cleanup releases an owner a `;` defer kept back only where it was left to the cleanup. Every exit of a function runs fn_cleanup, which released each owner in the defer snapshots (`__dsnap_udrop__`) on every path: also on a `^` above the owner's binding (never bound), on a `^` between the binding and the `;` (released by that exit, compiled before the snapshot held the owner), and around an arm that held the owner and its defer (the owner's own drop flag is written by the binding, which did not run). Each kept owner with a destructor now has a flag (`__dsnap_keep__`, an `i1` cleared at entry by emit_hoisted), set exactly where an exit's drain or a scope-end drain skips the owner because a snapshot holds it (`mem_dkeep_mark` in `mem_drop_user_drops`, `mem_drop_new_user_drops`, `mem_arm_exit_open`, and at the end of a defer body for what the body bound), and fn_cleanup releases the owner under it (`__dkeep_guard_open`) (agora #630) | §7.1 | no: releases only. | A handover by name (`: T b a`, `= b a`) out of a String, a Vec or a struct binding that owns its value is a move in the generated code: the target takes the source's drop flag where the handover runs and the source drops nothing until it is given a new value (`amv_open`, `__mem_udrop_bind_flag`). It made the target a cursor over the source, which came to own the value at a later `=` of the source that found it among the source's cursors, the ones made lexically before that `=`: in a loop (`= t a  = a b  = b t`) a cursor made later in the body, in the round before, was missed, and its value was dropped under it. The walk leaves the source of such a row (`am`) moved also where it finds it may own its value on some paths only, and handing that one over is refused as handing over a borrow. An owned slice assigned to another owned slice (`= ys zs`) moves its buffer where it runs: `zs`' slot is left empty and `zs` is moved for the walk (`__slice_asn_moves`); it was taken off a compile-time owner list for the rest of the enclosing arm or loop body only (agora #634) | §2.2, §7.6 | no: copy/take/release, and made to reject more. The move is the take §2.2 states (`= b a` moves an owner); a handover by name out of a binding that may own its value on some paths only is refused where it was a cursor, and a read of an owned slice after an assignment moved its buffer is refused where it read the moved buffer (freed by the target's next assignment) | none to the proof: A2's `move` takes the owner out of the source's slot at the step that runs it, which is what the generated code now does on every path; no lemma depends on the cursor reading |

Two things it leans on are not rules of the Lean model, and are stated. (1) A phase-C reading. In A3 a copy of a handle is a new share of the same block (`arcClone`, `lockClone`, `cellClone`), and the model types a change through a share that is a root — an owner or a borrowed parameter (`StmtT.withLock`, A3.2; `Rhs.atomic` and `Rhs.chanSend` / `chanRecv` / `chanClose` take the share lent, A3.4). A share read out of a field, an element or a payload — `( locked_update ( arc_get . x rows ) … )`, `. app jobs` handed to a function — is no root there. nurlc's form is read as the share copied out of its holder into a binding of its own (a new share of the same block: it takes nothing from the holder and changes nothing in it), the change made through that binding, and the binding ended. Phase C owes that reading; it is not yet in RULES.md's table. (2) An assumption, the one row two extends. The handles the standard library asserts `% Sync` and keeps in an rcbox are the model's cells (A3.5), which are never Sync: that every change through one is ordered by the handle is the library's assertion about its own raw code — one of the marker assertions in CLAIM.md's trusted base — trusted until now for a handle captured directly, and now also for the same handle reached by a by-value pass. For an `Arc` and the lock kinds nothing is assumed: the change is the block's, under its lock, not the lender's binding's, and the checker no longer counts it against the binding the share was read out of |
| A program's own `unsafe % Sync T { }` (generic or per instance; `% NotSync ( T A )` takes one instance back) is trusted for the same pass: a change made through a `T` borrowed by value, through any number of calls, is taken to be synchronised by `T`. What the author of the mark vouches for: every change reachable through a by-value copy of a value of this type is synchronised by the type itself. A by-value copy of a struct is a copy of its handles (any copy of a `Vec` / `String` handle reaches the one control block, agora #295), so a `% Sync` struct with a plain `Vec` field vouches for that `Vec` too. The answer is asked of the concrete type of the instance compiled (`__thr_orders_itself`), so one instance's mark does not answer for another (agora #639) | §3, §6.5 | no: the model has no marks of a program's own; this is the unsafe boundary | an assumption, extended: the mark was trusted for "a value of this type captured directly may be changed through its API from several threads", and is now trusted also for the same value reached by a by-value pass. | Two summaries the borrow rules read became exact; what the rules ask is unchanged. (1) An edge of a function's summary noted at several calls of one callee keeps each call's condition as an alternative and is there where any of them holds (`__cedge_add`, resolved by `resolve_cond_edges` at module end). The conditions used to be narrowed to the conjuncts the calls shared, and to `always` where they shared none: a function reporting an error from two places, with two differently built values, was summarised as handing its parameter on. (2) What a value bound to a call of a function not summarised yet is read out of waits for that function's summary (`__pderiv_note`: a condition on the callee's lends, resolved at module end through the conditional edges of agora #461). It used to be every argument at once — the lend list of a callee nothing is known of names them all — so a copy made by a helper defined below its caller held the parameter's storage. What is compared: at a call of a function already summarised, the value is read out of the arguments `mem_fn_lent_params` names — the handle, lend, part and read records of the result, and the returned-parameter summary `g_fn_ret_param`, minus what the callee sinks or keeps; at a call of one not summarised yet, out of each parameter the argument expressions lead to, where the callee's final `mem_fn_lend_sources` names that argument — the same records without `g_fn_ret_param`, the stack-reference summary of §2.8, which for a heap handle handed back names what the handle record names and for a number names a copy. Every verdict that reads either is given at module end, when both are final. The same holds on both sides of the thread rules. In a function, each path a value reaches a parameter by keeps its own condition (`#pdc:<ptr>` holds one `p#c` word per path, alternatives; a forward callee handed a binding that was itself read out of a parameter is under both conditions, joined), and a `??` payload's binding leads where the scrutinee matched leads, a field of a parameter among them (`__pderiv_reach`). In a closure several threads run, what a local of the body was read out of through a forward call is a conditional derivation as well (`g_clo_deriv_c`, resolved with the closure's effects at module end), with one fallback: a body that gives a conditionally derived local a new value (a plain `=`, a non-scalar field assignment, an `inout` argument, an inner closure's assignment replayed; `g_clo_cond_off`) drops the conditions and is judged as before the change — every forward call hands back what it was handed (agora #639) | §2.5, §2.7, §2.10 | no: the rules are unchanged (`CallT` against the callee's summary; a borrow ends where its root may reallocate). The summaries are nurlc's own, and the premise of phase C's readings | none to the proof. What it touches is RULES.md's premise `a reading holds only where nurlc's summary of the callee is at least what the callee does` (agora #315, #461): both summaries are over-approximations made tighter, and stay over-approximations. An edge belongs to the call that makes it, under that call's condition, so the disjunction of the calls' conditions holds wherever the edge exists; a derivation through a forward call is resolved against the callee's final lends. The corpus's rejected probes for the conditioned edges (agora #315, #461) are refused as before, and a callee defined below its caller — alone, through a second one, mutually recursive; lending an element, a field, the parameter itself; and making a copy — is a probe each way (`…_lent_by_a_function_defined_below_it` refused, `…_its_own_copy_made_by_a_function_defined_below_it_runs_clean` accepted), on the closure side and the `Arc` side (`shared_closure_grows_an_option_field_payload_lent_back_by_a_function_defined_below`, `arc_payload_reader_grows_its_own_copies_of_a_bound_element_and_an_option_field_payload_runs_clean`). | A declared type's or trait's name holds no `__` past its leading underscores and does not end in `_`, and an import's alias holds no `__`, does not end in `_` and is not the name of a type or trait the program declares (nor may a type declared later take an alias's name). `__` is the compiler's own separator: `( Arc Bag )` is spelled `Arc__Bag`, `( Vec i )` `Vec__i64`, and a module's `Point` imported as `geo` is `geo__Point`. Every rule that asks what a type is (which library handle, which generic, whose instance, what its fields are) reads the name's base, and the base is the declared name whole: its leading underscores, and an import alias with the `__` after it, up to the separator where the type arguments start (`__lty_base_end`, the one reader behind `__lty_base_name`, `__lty_base_is` and `__name_is_inst`). What makes the reading unambiguous is held at the declarations: a struct, enum or trait whose name holds the separator, or ends in `_` (`( Holder_ i )` is `Holder___i64`, a `Holder` of `_i64`), is refused where it stands (`__type_name_guard`; a module's declarations behind an alias are judged by the part after the alias), and the alias where it is written (`__import_alias_check`). `_private`, `__internal`, `my_geo` and `Arc_Bag` are names of a program's own. Before, the key was dropped inside the recursion with its node still counting the slot (`mem_take` had emptied it), and the tree's own drop ran the destructor again on the empty slot. No checker rule changes: what is accepted and refused is the same (agora #658) | §7.2 | no: a library change (stdlib/std/btree.nu). `BTree` is outside the calculus; the change restores, for this operation, what the model takes for granted of a container: a value is dropped once | none to the proof |

An entry leaves the container before its destructor runs — a map's or a set's slot becomes a tombstone and the count goes down, a deque's run leaves its count, a tree's kid is unlinked and its node's key count goes down past the entry — so the container holds exactly the entries still to go whenever a destructor runs; and while the release runs, a record of the releasing frame (`MapRelease`, `SetRelease`, `DqRelease`, `BtRelease`: a library handle over the same control block, forgotten when the release returns) is in the journal. | An owned slice binding's ownership is a run-time drop flag wherever it depends on the path: a `: ~` slice from its `:`, a binding of a join of a fresh slice and a borrowed one by the join's bit, a binding of a call that answers per call by its answer. An immutable `:` of an owned slice moves it; a returned join of own slices hands the selected one over; a returned slice field of a local struct is taken (agora #664, #540) | §7.6 | no: §7.6's single-owner rule, kept per path where membership in a static list could not | none |
| A call argument whose type may hold closures (a struct with a closure field, an option or enum payload of one, a Vec of them) passes the captures of the closures it holds to the bindings it is stored into, as a closure argument does (agora #665, #718, `__cc_note_call`): `( vec_push hv hd )` with `hd` a Holder of a closure teaches `hv` its captures, so `( arc_new hv )` asks the Sync-closure rule of them and a thread reading `hv` asks Send of them. A field assignment adds the stored closure's captures to the binding (`__clo_field_cs`) | §6.5 | no: a value's captures are part of the value, wherever it is stored; the model's `arc t` / `spawn` read the captures of every closure a value holds, which nurlc now carries through these stores | extends: rejects more |
| A handover by name (`: T b a`, `= b a`) out of a binding that owns its value on some paths only (`__sborrow` `dyn` or `@slot`), or of a value of any type with a drop slot (an option, a struct, an enum), is a move: the target takes the source's run-time drop flag, the source's flag is cleared, and the walk's row says so (amv_open, `:m`). The source is maybe-moved where it may have owned it (refused if read before a new value) and loses nothing where it borrowed. In `unsafe` and trusted code the generated code makes the same move; only the walk's row is not told. Before, the `dyn` String kept the cursor path, the option's walk row said copy while codegen moved the flag, and unsafe code kept the cursor, where the #634 rotation read freed memory (agora #667 items 1, 2, 4) | §2.2 | yes: `= b x ( = d x )` with x maybe-owned is refused at the read of x. Newly accepted: the #634 rotation out of a maybe-owned source (each name's flag is its run-time ownership; tree_sweep) | none to the proof: per-path ownership (DynCell), `moveOwn` where the source owns and `copyBor` where it lends; the walk refuses the source after the handover on every path, stricter than both |
| A handover by name keeps the source's journal entry when the drain reads the source's drop flag for it (a handle's `push_drop2`, `__ujs`, `__tmpj` — __jrnl_by_flag): the flag is the one the source's scope end drops by. Before, the entry was forgotten at the handover and nothing registered it again when the source was given a value, so that value leaked under recover (agora #667 item 3) | §7 | no: codegen only (a forget not emitted) | none to the proof: the journal entry mirrors the drop flag, as the scope end does |
| `= o e` where `e` was bound from a value block whose tail is o (`: e { o }`, `: e { { o } }`; `__btail`) is asked by its key, as a cursor over o is: on the runs `e` holds o's own value, o keeps it and nothing is released. Before, o's value was released and stored back freed (agora #667) | §7.6 | no: codegen only (a run-time key compare) | none to the proof: assigning a value to its own owner is the identity |
| A `: ~` binding of a value block's tail binding (`: ~ e { : T s … s }`) is that binding to its drop flag, as the `:` one is (#615): a cursor given a value of its own later hands over only that one, by its per-run own flag (#781), so the owner link may stand (agora #668) | §7.6 | no: §7.6 already says a moved value leaves its owner and a cursor's owner keeps its own | none |
| A `~` loop element whose type has a `% Drop` of its own and cannot be copied, handed to a `sink` parameter, is refused (its slot cannot be emptied: the container would run the destructor on the emptied value); one that can be copied is handed a copy. And `^ b` of a local binding that may hold a lend (`= b x`, x a loop element or a vec_at binding) answers per call whether it lends when its type cannot be copied (mem_hown_mark_dyn), as one that can be copied already did through the copy; before, it answered "owned" and the caller released a value its owner still held (agora #669) | §7.6 | no: §7.6 already forbids handing over what another owner drops and says a lend is answered as a lend; nurlc now applies both to `% Drop` values that cannot be copied | none |
| A `;` defer inside a loop body runs at a way out above it, and the walk reads it there with what the earlier round left (agora #670). Every exit of a defer loop branches to one label at the loop's end, which branches to the defer chain's top as it is there (`__dexit_top`, `__dloop_close`): the armed flags decide what runs, so a defer further down the loop than a `^` or a `\` runs there from the second round on. The walk carries the bindings such a defer keeps (`__bck_loop_kept`: every `let` in scope at a defer site of the body, above it — what codegen keeps) across the back-edge and the loop's exit with their borrows, does not end them at the body's end, and lets go of what borrowed from an earlier round's value when the name is bound again (`__bck_kept_rebind`: that value is parked to the cleanup); the exit's replay (`bck_exit_maybe_defers`, `bck_defers_release_ret`, `bck_end_post_defer`) reads a defer further down a loop around it. The owner a `^` hands back is the binding its row names (an arm's shadowing `g'1`), not the one its bare name first had | §2.10, §5.3, §7.2 | no: rejects more — a defer that reads, at a way out above it, an earlier round's view whose source (bound before the loop) this round changed, a defer that frees what a `^` above it hands back, and a change after the loop to what the last round's view borrows from. One acceptance: a `^` handing back an arm's own binding while a defer frees an outer binding of that name (the defer never reaches the returned value) | A2 has no `defer`; the calculus's claim covers defer bodies read as code at the function's exits, the bindings they read living to the function's end — this change makes codegen run a loop's defer at every exit the earlier rounds armed it for, and the walk check those reads with the values the rounds left |
| A `~` loop's element that a `;` defer of the loop's body reads is a borrow of the container's owners (its `fe_`) from the loop's end to the function's exit: bck_borrow_begin at the loop-block row (__bck_fe_defer_keep), and an enclosing loop keeps it across its back-edge and exit as it keeps a `:` binding of its body (__bck_loop_kept). The defer runs at the exit and reads the element's slot there; ended with its loop, a change to the container after the loop went unchecked and the defer read freed memory (agora #672). In codegen a defer body sees no slot of an element its loop bound (`__eslot_dd`, __fe_has_slot): the round's registers are no values at the exit, so it reads the element from its own binding, and its walk refuses a hand-over of it or of its field (a borrow; a `pendfx` row) | §2.10, §5.3 | no rule of the model: §2.10's borrow of an element, kept as long as the defer that reads it, as §5.3 keeps a `:` binding a defer reads (agora #491). It refuses more: only a program whose defer reads a loop element after the container changed (no file of main's tree) | none to the proof: the walk keeps the element the borrow the calculus's `vec_at` element already is |
| The Send/Sync derivation reads what it is asked about, not a name: a generic struct's instance through the fields and enum payloads the instance has (they are registered under the instance's own name, and the walk looked under the generic's bare name and found none — `( Wrap i )` of `: Wrap [T] { ( Rc T ) held }` crossed a thread); every type argument of a generic as the type that was written (an option was a slug of the mangled name the walk could not take apart — `( Vec ?( Rc i ) )` crossed); an `ArcWeak T` as the `Arc T` it upgrades to, to both questions (T Send and Sync, at every depth; and an `ArcWeak` in an `Arc`'s payload is that Arc, not an unsynchronised share handle); an `Rc`'s `Weak` as the `Rc` it upgrades to (neither Send nor Sync). A `% NotSync` on an instance outranks an `unsafe % Sync` on its generic in every rule that asks whether a type synchronises itself (the frozen `Arc` payload's excuse, the by-value change summary, the frozen walk, the `Arc`-backed / `Rc`-backed handle tests), as it already did in the crossing checks. The derivation, and the frozen-`Arc` rule's walk for a value that holds an `Arc`, read a type whole: every type it reaches is asked once, and the set of types asked is what ends the walk on a type that holds itself — a budget of ten levels answered "nothing found" below it (an `Rc` eleven structs down crossed a thread; an `Arc` eleven structs down lent its payload for writing). The holder walk keeps an answer only for a type whose walk was whole: a provisional "holds none" for a type still being read was final to a type that holds it back (agora #675, #683, #690, #693, #711) | §6.5 | no rule of the model. It refuses more (the tree sweep refuses no file of main; the newly refused programs are the PR's own probes and diag tests) and accepts more in three places where main refused by how it read a name: a type argument after an `Arc` / `RwLocked` first argument was held to Sync with that argument's payload (`( Two ( Arc i ) Cell )` moved); an instance's `unsafe % Send` / `unsafe % Sync` marker was found only on the last argument of the generic around it; the library's `ArcWeak` in an `Arc`'s payload was taken for a share handle with one unsynchronised state (`: Node { ?( ArcWeak Node ) parent }` in an `Arc`) | none to the proof: the model has no generics, no marker traits and no name-keyed tables; its threads rule is stated over the types a value holds, which is what nurlc now reads |
| A lending call bound over several lines — an argument on a later line than the binding's head, or the whole call on the next line — lends to the binding its statement binds, as the same call on one line does (agora #678): a pending row's lend is keyed by its statement's line (`__bck_pend_line`, field 8, `__bck_pl_line`), no longer by the line its argument was read on, which a binding's `let` row never carries; the lends a call's later lines note are one statement's and kept together. Ending the source afterwards (`vec_clear`, a growth, a drop) is refused where it was accepted, and the binding read freed memory | §2.10 | no: a borrow of the call's lent argument is the calculus's (`CallT`, `derive`) whatever the layout; the line was the checker's key alone | extends: rejects more |
| The calls of a returned expression are judged before its `ret` row (agora #680): the rows the walk decides at its end (`ppends`: a field of a borrow handed to a `sink` — `pendfx` —, a keeping call, a call's lend) were recorded behind the `ret` row, which ends the path, and never walked; they are recorded before it now, after an `expr` row that reads what the return read, as a statement's are (`__bck_ret_flush_ppends`; the views-itself case did so already, agora #584). `^ ( keep . c name )`, `^ + 1 ( keep . c name )` and the same call as the implicit tail are refused where they freed a field the owner still held, as the call bound first already was. What the return itself moves into the value it hands back stays the `ret` row's (a lend, where it reads through a borrow) | §2.10 | no: a field taken from a borrow is `RhsT.moveOwn` of what is no owner, refused in the calculus wherever it stands | extends: rejects more |
| A field of a call's result that the read takes over under the call's flag (`__last_tmp_field_own__`: a user enum, a handle that can be copied), matched in place, lends its payloads by that call's final summaries, the `&callee%args` form a matched call's payloads take (agora #681, gen_match `__m_tkey`): `?? . ( arc_get [App] mine ) ev { Got p → … }` makes `p` a view of `mine`'s payload, so changing it is refused by the frozen-Arc rule as for the option field beside it, and `?? . ( vec_at [App] apps 0 ) ev { Got p → … }` makes it a borrow of `apps`. A field of a fresh call's result stays the arm's own; a payload only read stays accepted | §2.10, §6.5 | no: a payload of what a call lends is `CallT`'s view of the call's source, whichever field the read took it out of | extends: rejects more |
| A view of an Arc's payload stays one through the owners it is kept in (agora #682): a Vec it is pushed into, a map it is put in, a struct a Vec holds, a struct a helper builds over its Arc parameter. Such an owner is marked holding one (`ah_`, set by the store, by an owner bound to a value holding one, and by a copy of it). What is lent back out of it is one: a `??` payload, a value a call lends out of it whose type can hold a view (`vec_at`, `vec_get`, `map_get`, a helper's element), a field of it, a borrow of it. The frozen-payload rule then refuses a change through it, as for the view bound directly. A struct's view field given one after the struct was built makes the struct one (`ab_`), as its literal does. The owner itself is not one: pushing into it, truncating or clearing it, moving it changes the owner, not the payload | §6.5 | no: the model's frozen `arc t` refuses a change through every view of the payload, wherever the view is kept; nurlc now follows the view through the owners that hold it | extends: rejects more |
| A closure several threads may run (the Sync-closure rule, docs/MEMORY.md §7.7) that hands a FIELD of a struct it captured, reached through fields held by value, to a callee taking it by value, is judged by that field's type, not the capture's (agora #684, __clo_mut_atoms / __clo_fpath_ty): `( arc_clone [Pl] . a ap )` with `ap` an `( Arc Pl )` changes only the share count, atomically, as for an Arc captured itself (accepted before). An `inout` or `sink` parameter is the holder's storage or takes the field out of it, and stays a change of the capture (agora #639); a field behind a pointer or in a container's element is judged by the capture as before | §7.7 | no: the field is a handle that orders itself, which the rule already admits as a capture; only the path to it is new, and the path's struct fields are the capture's own storage, read, not changed | relaxes: accepts more |
| A closure's effects on its captures — what it changes, frees, gives a new value, the captures it holds — are those of every literal its closure node may be, read at each run (`__clo_run_attr`, agora #686): gen_closure_expr records them under the literal (`le#` / `lf#` / `la#` / `lc#`), and a run through an alias, an alias of an alias, an assignment, a `?` / `??` / block value, a literal chosen by an arm, one assigned in an arm, or a function it is handed to (which counts as running it) ends the views of the capture as the direct call does. A run in a loop through a node that gains a literal below it also runs that literal's effects at the body's end, before the back edge (`__loop_clo_replay`): the next round runs it. A closure that only reads its capture keeps the views through every route | §2.10 | no: a closure's run is `CallT` of its body over its captures, whichever binding names the closure; nurlc read the effects off the name its literal was bound to and now reads them off the closure node, which follows the copies the model's values do | extends: rejects more |
| A `( mem_dup x )` copy as an arm of a `?` join is owned by the join's result: gen_mem_dup notes the call depth it made the copy at (`g_dup_nest`, the depth __gen_call_nested keeps, reset at every expression), and an arm that is that call — or a block ending in it — one call below the join answers "owned" (__arm_is_mem_dup, read by mem_arm_hown0 for String / Vec arms and mem_arm_struct_hown for owning-struct arms) before any callee answer. A copy made inside a call the arm makes (`( f ( mem_dup x ) )`) is that call's argument, and the arm answers by f as before | §1, §7.6 | no: codegen only. A copy is a fresh owner, and a join's result owns what its taken arm owns | none to the proof: nurlc's join now hands over the owner the calculus already has there |
| An argument that is a call's result is traced to what lent it when its type holds storage a callee can change in place — a handle, or a value with a drop of its own that cannot be copied and holds an address, a `% Drop` struct with a Vec field (agora #692, `__arg_holds_changeable`; the gates asked `__is_handle_ty`, which answers no for such a struct): `( rg ( vec_at [R] rs 0 ) )` ends the views of the element's buffer as the Drop-less twin does, a helper doing it has the summary edge, and the frozen-Arc rule sees it through `arc_get`.
