A read-only callee ends nothing | §2.10, §6.5 | no: a lent value handed to a callee that changes it is `CallT`'s change of what lent it, whatever the value's drop | extends: rejects more |
| `= io . x f` into an `inout` parameter copies the field read when its root is any binding — a parameter the function only borrows, an `inout` one, a cursor over one, a global — as it did for a local the frame owns, and when its root is a call's value where the call lent it (its `__last_tmp_field_own__` bit, as `^ . ( f ) x` reads it); a type with a `% Drop` and no clone is refused there. Before, only a field of a value this frame owns was copied, so the caller's binding and the field's owner shared one buffer (agora #697) | §2.4, §2.12 | no rule of the model: an `inout` place owns what it holds (`paramOK_inout`), and a field read of a borrow is a `copyBor` the calculus copies before a store | none to the proof: codegen now makes the copy the calculus's store already assumes. It refuses more only for a `% Drop` field with no clone (no file of main's tree) |
| A function handing back a `dyn` method's result lends what the method's impls lend (agora #699): the provenance pass that computes a function's lends at module end (resolve_raw_provenance) found the call's virtual callee (`m__dyn__T`) lending nothing — its summaries, the union of the impls', are joined only afterwards (resolve_pending_impls' __dyn_virtual_round). In that pass the virtual's rows are now the union of its impls' as their records have them (__rpg_dyn_refresh: what they lend, borrow, point into, let escape), kept up to date as the impls' summaries grow, its callers solved again; and a returned dyn call is a summarised call (`dynvm##`). A caller that clears the source before reading the forwarder's result is refused, as the direct dyn call already was | §2.10, §6.4 | no: a call through an interface takes its lends from the interface, which `Implements` holds every impl to (`callc`) | extends: rejects more |
| A `??` join publishes its scrutinee call's lend key (`__last_join_lend_key__`, read by mem_ret_join_own) only when an arm may hand the scrutinee's payload back as it is: an arm that is no call, a call whose lend is exactly the scrutinee's pair, a `( mem_dup x )` of a view holder. When every arm that yields a value is a call whose own answer says what it lends (a fresh value, a copy, a lend of its own arguments — m_alends, resolved at module end), the key is empty (gen_match, m_allfresh). Before, `^ ?? ( vec_get p 0 ) { T v → ( string_clone v ) F → … }` was summarised as lending p (agora #702) | §2.8 | yes: it accepts more — a result taken for a borrow of the scrutinee's source is the caller's own where no arm hands the payload back; an arm lending it through its own call still lends (m_alends) | none to the proof: the summary was conservative; a fresh value has no dependency on the scrutinee |
| A `??` payload begins each run of its arm borrowing nothing: nurlc tells bindings apart by name, and the borrow relation (`bs_`) an earlier payload of the same name left was what the join that hands a payload's handle on read (`maybealias`: "its borrow relation, not its state, says what it was"). An owned payload assigned to an outer binding (`= bytes v`, a value join's `T v → v`) made that binding a borrow of the earlier scrutinee, and handing it on was refused; with either payload renamed the program was accepted. A lent payload's relation begins at the arm's entry as before, and an outer binding the payload shadows gets its own back after the arm (agora #703) | §2.2 | yes: made to accept more — exactly the programs that were accepted under another payload name | none to the proof: the model's binders are distinct (a name is one binding), so the refused program was well-typed there all along; the refusal came from nurlc's per-name table, which now agrees with the model |
| A closure binding declared a write-back (`mem_put_back`, a raw primitive) is stored into an element slot as it is, as a binding of every other type is: it was read out of storage and returns to it, in its own slot or another (two elements changing places). Before, a closure element store copied every binding's environment (`mem_clo_into_store`), `mem_put_back` or not, so a container moving closures through a binding (a `HashMap`'s rehash, `vec_swap`, `vec_reverse`, `vec_retain`, `sort_by`, a `Heap`'s sift) left a clone in the new slot and the original environment with no owner: a leak, one per element moved. The library's moves declare their elements write-backs now. No checker rule changes: what is accepted and refused is the same (agora #706) | §7.5 | no: a codegen change for a raw primitive and a library change (stdlib/core/vec.nu, stdlib/std/hashmap.nu, set.nu, sort.nu, heap.nu, btree.nu). A container's element moves are outside the calculus (raw code); a closure's environment has one owner again after each of them, which is what the model's closure values assume | none to the proof |
| A call's result passed straight as an argument, which the caller releases after the call, is the caller's for the whole call: it is journaled for that window with its type's own journal thunk (`mem_journal_push_temp_fn`, `__jthunk_of`; flag: owned and not kept by the callee) and forgotten where the caller drops it (`j\|slot`). Before, only a handle temporary was; a `% Drop` struct or an option of one was in no journal entry, and a panic in the callee never ran its destructor. No checker rule changes (agora #707) | §7.2 | no: codegen only. The calculus has no unnamed argument temporary: it binds every argument it owns (`letv` / `letd`), and a bound argument's frame runs its destructor on a panic in the callee; nurlc now does the same for the temporary it makes | none to the proof |
| What a container's release drops leaves the container's storage before the first destructor runs. `vec_clear`, `vec_truncate`, `vec_retain` and `deque_clear`, for an element type whose drop runs code of the program's own (`mem_drop_runs_code`), move the elements to go into a buffer of their own (the runtime frame's stack where they fit, the heap past that — Agora #748; the container's count short and its buffer kept), and drop them from there in index order (`mem_move_drop` → `nurl_vec_move_drop_dtor`; the release's record owns a heap buffer and frees it at its end, a panic's drain included). A destructor that reaches the container — pushes into it, grows it, clears it — then shares no slot with the release. Before, the elements were dropped out of the container's own buffer, its count already short: a push wrote over the next element to go (never dropped), the release dropped the pushed element while the container held it too, and a push that grew the buffer freed the storage the release walked (heap-use-after-free). Other element types keep the in-place release, instruction for instruction. No checker rule changes (agora #709) | §7.2 | no: a library and runtime change (stdlib/core/vec.nu, stdlib/std/deque.nu, stdlib/runtime_core.c) and one raw primitive. A container's release is outside the calculus; the change restores what the model takes for granted of one: each element dropped once, by the one release that holds it | none to the proof |
| The same-Rc rule (a call that may drop, replace or move what an `( Rc T )` reaches ends the borrows through every other handle that may be the same value) asks which Rc values two names may share by the SETS of Rc types they reach, where it compared one type per name by its spelling. What a call may change through an argument is everything the argument's type reaches (`__rc_reach`: fields by the instance's layout, enum payloads, inline members, type arguments, an Rc's payload — an Rc held in the payload of an Rc, and a `Weak` as the `Rc` it upgrades to), joined with what the shares it borrows from reach; a buffer it may MOVE is in its own storage unless the call was handed a value lent out of it. A view's source is a view of an Rc's payload where the source is, or holds in its own storage, that Rc (`__rc_direct`: fields at any depth, a Box's content — not a container's buffer, not an Rc's payload). The two meet or do not. On main a struct holding a share was another type than the share (`%H` vs `%Rc__…`), a Vec of shares or a payload of shares no Rc type at all, and an Rc two structs down none. The rule also asks the callee's irealloc summary (a callee that moves the buffer of an ELEMENT of what it is handed — `vec_reserve` of `( vec_at ( rc_get a ) 0 )` — ends what points into the elements of the other handle's payload), and so does the rule for a change made through a borrow (`( growelem q )`, q a `vec_at` of a Vec of Vecs: a level below the borrow's own buffer). A call handed a borrow of the very source is that borrow's own rows' for it, unless the borrow's own type reaches an Rc the source is or holds (a copy of a handle read out of an element is another handle on it). A library dropper or element replacer handed a value whole (`vec_clear`, `vec_put` of a Vec of shares) drops handles, which a view through another handle — a count of its own — outlives: what it changes of a payload is the value's own storage's, unless dropping an element runs a `% Drop` of the program's (agora #712, #497, #592, #601 item 2) | §2.10 | yes: made to reject more (a view through one handle read after a call changed the same Rc value through another: a struct holding a share, a Vec of shares, a share a call hands back, a payload holding shares, a share two structs down, an element grown by a helper) | extends: rejects more; in A2/A3 every share of a block reaches the same payload, and what reaches the block — a field, an element, a payload — reaches it through the share it holds: a change through any of them kills what depends on the block through any share. The type sets are nurlc's over-approximation of "may hold a share of the same block" |
| A closure parameter that a function runs on several threads at once — captured by a detach made inside a loop, or by two detaches, or handed on to a callee that does so (by summary, `syncrun##` / `syncreq##`) — holds the caller's closure to Sync as a closure kept in an Arc is (`__sync_verdict`: its captures are Sync, and it neither changes nor gives away what it captured), not to Send alone (agora #713, resolve_send_checks' seeds). `( par_map_index [i] n 8 f )` with `f` pushing to a captured Vec, a helper spawning a thread per round with its closure parameter, two spawns of it, and a forwarder to par_map_index are refused; a helper that spawns one thread with it still accepts a closure that changes its capture, and an `f` that only reads stays accepted | §6.5 | no: the model's `spawn` sends a closure to one thread, and a closure several threads run is the model's Sync closure (the `arc` of a closure value); nurlc now asks the second where the function runs the parameter on several threads | extends: rejects more |
| A thread rule's library leaves are the library's types, asked by what the type is (`__ty_std_named`), not by the spelling of its name (agora #714, as #639 for a handle named `Arc`): the compile-time Send/Sync walk's `Rc` and `Cell` leaves and its `Arc` / `RwLocked` share switch (`__thr_node`), and the run-time crossing check's `Shared`, `Rc` and `Weak` skips (`__sk_walk_calc`). A program's own struct or generic named `Weak` that holds a share is walked by its fields and counted at the crossing, so a share left behind panics there as it does for the same struct under another name; a program's own `Rc` of numbers is Send, and its own generic `Arc` moves its arguments as any struct does | §6.5 | no rule of the model. ### Audit of h426–h974 (agora #104, 2026-10-30)

The rows from agora #165 to #214 and #205 were written in one pass,
against main `20b200d` and `10b54e3`. In every one of these changes the diff to `docs/MEMORY.md`
only moved the probe ranges. No rule text changed: each change brought
nurlc into line with a rule that already held, or fixed the lowering.
Three findings matter to the proof:

- **No change made a rule accept more than A1 / A2 do.** Only two changes
  accept more: the address-free `??` payload (#206) and the field-name
  capture (#214). Each accepts exactly what the calculus already types
  (`projE … .int = intE`; `CapsOK`).
- **Copies are calls.** "Copied where it lent" (#178–#200, #202) is a call
  of the value's clone, which A2 types as a call with an owned result. A
  clone of a `Vec` of integers can even be written in the calculus
  (`RhsT.at`, `StmtT.push`). The clones' bodies are part of the standard
  library's base (B). A value with no clone is refused, as A2 refuses
  returning or storing the lend.
- **Per-call ownership is inside the calculus, through an encoding.**
  #184, #185, #186, #203, #205 and #206 answer at run time whether a
  result is owned. A2 types none of them directly: a binding is owned or
  borrowed for every run. But such a value is, in the calculus, an owner
  cell plus a borrow. The cell is an `inout` option the caller provides
  and the callee fills when the value is fresh. The borrow reaches the
  cell's payload or the argument, and `lends` names both.
  `A2/DynCell.lean` types this encoding with A2's rules and applies
  `memory_safe` and `no_leak_end` to it, on the standard axioms
  (`pick_ok`, `main_safe`, `main_no_leak`). So these shapes rest on the
  proof for the rules. That nurlc's bit behaves as the cell does (the bit
  set exactly when the cell would be `some`, the drop and the move under
  it) is phase C / D, and until then the probes check it.
  `A2/DYN_DESIGN.md` gives the correspondence. It also says why a direct
  mode `.dyn` would reopen the invariant's root lemmas, which the
  encoding avoids.

Most of the holes in this range were in surface forms the calculus has
only as statements: `?` / `??` joins as values, value blocks and their
tails, `\` tries, `;` defers and fall-off values. Each probe family was
the same shape in another one of these forms.
